 Engine gaps that were fixed in this round:
  21. The cut planes did not process the wake-induced mixing: the full-flow empirical Gauss solver
      (Python's full_flow_empirical_gauss_solver, core.py:1410) was not ported, so the flow-field
      planes (the horizontal/y/cross plane) of every empirical Gauss case were
      computed with the sequential solver and missed the wake-induced mixing of the turbine-grid
      solve. The full-flow solver is now a port: the turbine-grid solve (grid resolution 3,
      Python's turbine_grid) produces the mixing field wim_field, and every source turbine's wake
      is evaluated over the plane with the summed mixing term.
  22. The planar grids did not follow Python's extents: the Rust
      calculate_horizontal_plane / calculate_y_plane / calculate_cross_plane did not take the
      bounds of the plane and the y/x planes used +/- 2 rotor diameters around the hub heights
      instead of Python's (0.001, 6 * max(z)). The three functions now take the optional
      (x_bounds, y_bounds) of Python's API, use Python's defaults, and the cut planes of the
      examples match Python exactly (verified with control_types_004, turbine_002,
      visualizations_002 and visualizations_003 against the Python reference).

 Still unaligned (and why):
   * layout_optimization_003, 005 and wind_resource_grid_003
       - The genetic random search of Python is time based (it runs until a wall-clock deadline),
         so its result is not reproducible even in Python; the Rust version runs a fixed number of
         generations and iterations (layout_optimization_001, 002, 003 and 005 are 1:1 ports: the
         random wind resource and the AEP of the initial layout match to 1e-6 and the SLSQP
         optimizer of 001/002 reaches Python's optimum).
       - wind_resource_grid_003 is a 1:1 port as well, but the speed ups of the wind resource
         grid are reproduced with a Bowyer-Watson triangulation (scipy uses qhull), so the
         heterogeneous map differs slightly.
   * The coupled layout optimization with the geometric yaw (layout_optimization_002): the
     objective is identical to Python's (the Rust version reproduces Python's 16515.9 MWh for
     Python's optimal layout), but the objective is not smooth (the geometric yaw angles jump
     when the nearest downstream turbine changes), so the two optimizers end up in different
     local optima (16126.9 MWh against 16515.9 MWh).
